             (progn
               (setf *item-locations*
                     '((zebra . player) (apple . player)))
               (not (null (search "apple and zebra"
                                  (string-downcase
                                   (inventory))))))
             t))
    (nreverse results)))
#+end_src

That's the reference game. The chapters' individual project
steps build it piece by piece; this is what they add up to.
The reader's version may differ in room descriptions, item
list, test set, and history limits — that's expected. The
structure is what matters.

* Appendix H: Glossary

Terms the standard itself defines, plus the ones this guide
introduced. Alphabetical.

| Term            | Definition                                                       |
|-----------------+------------------------------------------------------------------|
| atom            | Any value that isn't a cons — numbers, symbols, strings, etc.    |
| binding         | The association between a name and a value in a scope            |
| cdr             | The second part of a cons; the tail of a list                    |
| car             | The first part of a cons; the first element of a list            |
| class           | A CLOS type; the "template" for its instances                    |
| closure         | A function that captures bindings from an enclosing scope       |
| condition       | An object that represents a situation (error, warning, etc.)    |
| cons            | The fundamental pair structure; a cell with two parts            |
| destructive     | A function that modifies its argument; "n"-prefixed by convention |
| dynamic extent  | The time during which a form's bindings are in force             |
| form            | A Lisp expression; the unit of code                              |
| function        | A callable value; the function slot of a symbol                  |
| generic function| A named dispatch target; collects methods                        |
| instance        | An object built from a class                                     |
| keyword         | A self-evaluating symbol whose name begins with =:=              |
| lexical scope   | Scope determined by position in the source text                  |
| list            | A chain of conses ending in nil                                  |
| macro           | A function from forms to forms, run at compile time              |
| method          | One implementation of a generic function's behaviour             |
| multiple values | Several results returned together, distinguishable by binding    |
| package         | A namespace for symbols                                          |
| plist           | A flat alternating key/value list; on symbols, the property list |
| predicate       | A function returning true/false (conventionally =-p= suffixed)  |
| proper list     | A list ending in nil                                             |
| reader          | The parser that turns text into Lisp objects                     |
| restart         | A recovery choice offered at a signal site                       |
| sequence        | An ordered collection (list, string, vector)                     |
| sexp            | A single Lisp form (the reader's output)                         |
| shadow          | A package's own symbol for a name it would otherwise inherit     |
| special         | A dynamic variable; its binding follows the call stack           |
| symbol          | A named object with slots for value, function, and plist        |
| tail position   | The last expression a function evaluates before returning        |
| uninterned      | A symbol not in any package's table (e.g., from gensym=)        |
| value           | What a form evaluates to                                         |

* Appendix I: Concept Index

A guided index of the concepts each chapter covered, in
chapter order, so it doubles as a table of contents by idea.
It is not a name index: it is for looking up /ideas/.

** Part I: First Steps

- Ch 1: REPL loop; prefix notation; the edit-evaluate loop
- Ch 2: evaluation rule; =quote=; atoms vs lists; =nil= truth
- Ch 3: exact numbers (ratios); characters vs strings vs symbols; upper-casing
- Ch 4: =defparameter= vs =defvar=; =let= vs =let*=; earmuffs
- Ch 5: =defun=; =&optional=/=&key=/=&rest=; docstrings; the return-value rule
- Ch 6: =if=/=when=/=unless=/=cond=/=case=; truthiness (only nil is false); =and=/=or= short-circuit

** Part II: Thinking in Lists

- Ch 7: cons cells; alists; =car=/=cdr=; sharing
- Ch 8: recursion; base case; accumulator; =trace=
- Ch 9: functions as values; =#'=; closures; higher-order functions
- Ch 10: sequence functions; the four equalities; =sort= is destructive

** Part III: Core Tools

- Ch 11: =dolist=/=dotimes=/=loop=; =return= vs =return-from=
- Ch 12: =format= directives; =~{~}= iteration; conditional directives
- Ch 13: structures; hash tables; arrays; multiple values (first contact)
- Ch 14: =defmacro=; backquote; =gensym=; macro hygiene
- Ch 15: signalling; =handler-case=; =restart-case=; interactive restarts
- Ch 16: packages; files; ASDF; the =:serial= rule

** Part IV: The Lisp Way

- Ch 17: lexical vs dynamic scope; earmuffs as discipline; =*game-output*=
- Ch 18: the reader; =*readtable*=; =#== dispatch; =with-standard-io-syntax=
- Ch 19: =handler-bind=; =muffle-warning=; the condition hierarchy
- Ch 20: =compile-file=/=load=; three times; =eval-when=
- Ch 21: types as value-properties; =declare=/=declaim=; =optimize= qualities
- Ch 22: the REPL as workbench; =inspect=/=describe=/=break=; reading backtraces
- Ch 23: symbols as five-part records; plists; =get=/=getf= distinction
- Ch 24: multiple values; =multiple-value-bind=; the single-value context table
- Ch 25: closures as objects; shared environments; closure vs defparameter state
- Ch 26: CLOS; =defclass=/=defmethod=; =initialize-instance=
- Ch 27: method combination (=+=, =max=); =change-class=; =shared-initialize=
- Ch 28: package internals; =shadow=; the =:use=/=:import-from= distinction
- Ch 29: JSON/CSV interop; =uiop:run-program=; CFFI
- Ch 30: generators; the two-value protocol; lazy vs eager
- Ch 31: the reading habit; the library audit checklist
- Ch 32: =save-lisp-and-die=; =:toplevel=; the build script; exit codes
- Ch 33: test discipline; test the behaviour not the internals; one test per bug
- Ch 34: the ecosystem; the Hyperspec habit; "thinking in Lisp"

* Colophon: How the Guide Grew

This book was written as one continuous conversation, part by
part, against a running project that never used an idea
before the chapter that taught it. The core — Parts I through
III and the game's first rooms — came first; the later
chapters were added in response to an audit of what a working
Lisp programmer meets that the early chapters had left
implicit: the reader and the printer's knobs, multiple values,
closures as objects, CLOS beyond the basics, package
internals, generators, the disciplines of declarations, of
reading strangers' code, of interoperating, of shipping.

The running game — the Cave of Noise — carried every idea in
turn: alists for rooms, closures for history, conditions for
failsafes, CLOS for items, packages for the split, generators
for the streamed replay, and finally =save-lisp-and-die= for
the shipped executable. If you worked the project steps, you
built a program; if you worked the exercises, you built the
reflexes. The two together were the promise: not a reference
that tells, but a guide that holds your hand until the hand
lets go.

The tools that made it: Emacs, SBCL, Typst. The tools you
now know better than the person who configured your init
file did.
